Surah: 58. Follow the brief below (surah_map.md) exactly. The evidence is text.md (the surah), dictionary.md (every root of the surah's words, each branch with the classical dictionaries' own phrases), channels.md and hft.md (both are earlier readers' proposals: ignore their judgements (grades, strength or confidence labels, reading types, statements of what a reading may or may not do) and make your own) and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Return only the map as your final message.

===== _commentary/v16/prompts/r2/surah_map.md =====
Read the surah as one text and write its map of image chains: the lexical images that run through several of
its ayat and join them into one scene, process or movement. A later writer will read one ayah at a time, with
only that ayah's own dictionary. The map lets that writer hear what the ayah's words carry in the surah as a
whole, including senses whose evidence sits under the words of other ayat.

Your evidence is the surah text, the dictionary of every root in the surah (each branch with the classical
dictionaries' own phrases), earlier readers' channel review (channels.md) and activation hypotheses (hft.md),
and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Where a member or passage comes from memory rather than from
the dictionary or the text, say so. Do not use tools, delegate, browse or inspect files.

The channel review and the HFT records are proposals by earlier readers. Ignore their judgements: grades,
strength or confidence labels, reading types, words such as "surprising", "exploratory" or "latent", and every
statement of what a reading may or may not do. Make your own judgement from the surah's words, the dictionary
phrases and the Quran. Do not rediscover what they already assembled: start from their chains, test each one
against the dictionary phrases and the text, and join, extend, split or correct them. Where their wording
abstracts a member, go back to the dictionary phrase and name what it actually says.

A chain belongs on the map when its members are senses the dictionary attests for words that stand in the
surah, and together they make one image or process that the surah's wording or sequence lets a listener hear.
A member need not be the sense that translates its word in its own ayah; a chain is heard across the surah,
not in one word. Say plainly when a member is a rare sense or is attested only inside a fixed expression, and
keep it. When the dictionary itself joins two of the surah's words in one phrase, quote that phrase: it is
the strongest evidence a chain can have. Keep a scene at the level of its objects, their parts and their
operation. When proposals share members, do not fold one into another's more abstract function unless nothing
concrete is lost. Carry every chain that meets this test, however unusual; leave out proposals that do not.

Write the map in English, with Arabic quoted exactly (surah wording from the text, dictionary phrases from the
dictionary). It is working material for the writer, not commentary prose.

1. `## Chains`. For each chain, a `###` heading naming the image. One paragraph on what the image is and how it
   moves through the surah. Then its members, one line each: the ayah ref, the word as it stands in the text,
   the root and branch id, the dictionary's own phrase quoted exactly, and what this member contributes to the
   image. Add Quran passages outside the surah, with exact refs, where they stage or confirm the chain.
2. `## Ayat`. For each ayah in order: which chains it opens, advances or completes, and what its words add to
   each.
3. `## Not carried`. Each channel subchannel and each HFT record you did not carry into a chain, one line each:
   its name and why.

No ranking and no labels of strength or confidence. No list of what a writer must include. There is no length
target and no required number of chains or members.


===== _commentary/v16/work/s058/surah.r2/text.md =====
# Surah 58

- 58:1 قَدْ سَمِعَ ٱللَّهُ قَوْلَ ٱلَّتِى تُجَٰدِلُكَ فِى زَوْجِهَا وَتَشْتَكِىٓ إِلَى ٱللَّهِ وَٱللَّهُ يَسْمَعُ تَحَاوُرَكُمَآ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ سَمِيعٌۢ بَصِيرٌ
- 58:2 ٱلَّذِينَ يُظَٰهِرُونَ مِنكُم مِّن نِّسَآئِهِم مَّا هُنَّ أُمَّهَٰتِهِمْ ۖ إِنْ أُمَّهَٰتُهُمْ إِلَّا ٱلَّٰٓـِٔى وَلَدْنَهُمْ ۚ وَإِنَّهُمْ لَيَقُولُونَ مُنكَرًۭا مِّنَ ٱلْقَوْلِ وَزُورًۭا ۚ وَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَعَفُوٌّ غَفُورٌۭ
- 58:3 وَٱلَّذِينَ يُظَٰهِرُونَ مِن نِّسَآئِهِمْ ثُمَّ يَعُودُونَ لِمَا قَالُوا۟ فَتَحْرِيرُ رَقَبَةٍۢ مِّن قَبْلِ أَن يَتَمَآسَّا ۚ ذَٰلِكُمْ تُوعَظُونَ بِهِۦ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ خَبِيرٌۭ
- 58:4 فَمَن لَّمْ يَجِدْ فَصِيَامُ شَهْرَيْنِ مُتَتَابِعَيْنِ مِن قَبْلِ أَن يَتَمَآسَّا ۖ فَمَن لَّمْ يَسْتَطِعْ فَإِطْعَامُ سِتِّينَ مِسْكِينًۭا ۚ ذَٰلِكَ لِتُؤْمِنُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ ۚ وَتِلْكَ حُدُودُ ٱللَّهِ ۗ وَلِلْكَٰفِرِينَ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ
- 58:5 إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يُحَآدُّونَ ٱللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُۥ كُبِتُوا۟ كَمَا كُبِتَ ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِهِمْ ۚ وَقَدْ أَنزَلْنَآ ءَايَٰتٍۭ بَيِّنَٰتٍۢ ۚ وَلِلْكَٰفِرِينَ عَذَابٌۭ مُّهِينٌۭ
- 58:6 يَوْمَ يَبْعَثُهُمُ ٱللَّهُ جَمِيعًۭا فَيُنَبِّئُهُم بِمَا عَمِلُوٓا۟ ۚ أَحْصَىٰهُ ٱللَّهُ وَنَسُوهُ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ شَهِيدٌ
- 58:7 أَلَمْ تَرَ أَنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَعْلَمُ مَا فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَمَا فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ ۖ مَا يَكُونُ مِن نَّجْوَىٰ ثَلَٰثَةٍ إِلَّا هُوَ رَابِعُهُمْ وَلَا خَمْسَةٍ إِلَّا هُوَ سَادِسُهُمْ وَلَآ أَدْنَىٰ مِن ذَٰلِكَ وَلَآ أَكْثَرَ إِلَّا هُوَ مَعَهُمْ أَيْنَ مَا كَانُوا۟ ۖ ثُمَّ يُنَبِّئُهُم بِمَا عَمِلُوا۟ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ بِكُلِّ شَىْءٍ عَلِيمٌ
- 58:8 أَلَمْ تَرَ إِلَى ٱلَّذِينَ نُهُوا۟ عَنِ ٱلنَّجْوَىٰ ثُمَّ يَعُودُونَ لِمَا نُهُوا۟ عَنْهُ وَيَتَنَٰجَوْنَ بِٱلْإِثْمِ وَٱلْعُدْوَٰنِ وَمَعْصِيَتِ ٱلرَّسُولِ وَإِذَا جَآءُوكَ حَيَّوْكَ بِمَا لَمْ يُحَيِّكَ بِهِ ٱللَّهُ وَيَقُولُونَ فِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ لَوْلَا يُعَذِّبُنَا ٱللَّهُ بِمَا نَقُولُ ۚ حَسْبُهُمْ جَهَنَّمُ يَصْلَوْنَهَا ۖ فَبِئْسَ ٱلْمَصِيرُ
- 58:9 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِذَا تَنَٰجَيْتُمْ فَلَا تَتَنَٰجَوْا۟ بِٱلْإِثْمِ وَٱلْعُدْوَٰنِ وَمَعْصِيَتِ ٱلرَّسُولِ وَتَنَٰجَوْا۟ بِٱلْبِرِّ وَٱلتَّقْوَىٰ ۖ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ ٱلَّذِىٓ إِلَيْهِ تُحْشَرُونَ
- 58:10 إِنَّمَا ٱلنَّجْوَىٰ مِنَ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنِ لِيَحْزُنَ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ وَلَيْسَ بِضَآرِّهِمْ شَيْـًٔا إِلَّا بِإِذْنِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ وَعَلَى ٱللَّهِ فَلْيَتَوَكَّلِ ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ
- 58:11 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِذَا قِيلَ لَكُمْ تَفَسَّحُوا۟ فِى ٱلْمَجَٰلِسِ فَٱفْسَحُوا۟ يَفْسَحِ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمْ ۖ وَإِذَا قِيلَ ٱنشُزُوا۟ فَٱنشُزُوا۟ يَرْفَعِ ٱللَّهُ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ مِنكُمْ وَٱلَّذِينَ أُوتُوا۟ ٱلْعِلْمَ دَرَجَٰتٍۢ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ خَبِيرٌۭ
- 58:12 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِذَا نَٰجَيْتُمُ ٱلرَّسُولَ فَقَدِّمُوا۟ بَيْنَ يَدَىْ نَجْوَىٰكُمْ صَدَقَةًۭ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ خَيْرٌۭ لَّكُمْ وَأَطْهَرُ ۚ فَإِن لَّمْ تَجِدُوا۟ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌ
- 58:13 ءَأَشْفَقْتُمْ أَن تُقَدِّمُوا۟ بَيْنَ يَدَىْ نَجْوَىٰكُمْ صَدَقَٰتٍۢ ۚ فَإِذْ لَمْ تَفْعَلُوا۟ وَتَابَ ٱللَّهُ عَلَيْكُمْ فَأَقِيمُوا۟ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ وَءَاتُوا۟ ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ وَأَطِيعُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُۥ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ خَبِيرٌۢ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ
- 58:14 ۞ أَلَمْ تَرَ إِلَى ٱلَّذِينَ تَوَلَّوْا۟ قَوْمًا غَضِبَ ٱللَّهُ عَلَيْهِم مَّا هُم مِّنكُمْ وَلَا مِنْهُمْ وَيَحْلِفُونَ عَلَى ٱلْكَذِبِ وَهُمْ يَعْلَمُونَ
- 58:15 أَعَدَّ ٱللَّهُ لَهُمْ عَذَابًۭا شَدِيدًا ۖ إِنَّهُمْ سَآءَ مَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ
- 58:16 ٱتَّخَذُوٓا۟ أَيْمَٰنَهُمْ جُنَّةًۭ فَصَدُّوا۟ عَن سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ فَلَهُمْ عَذَابٌۭ مُّهِينٌۭ
- 58:17 لَّن تُغْنِىَ عَنْهُمْ أَمْوَٰلُهُمْ وَلَآ أَوْلَٰدُهُم مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ شَيْـًٔا ۚ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ أَصْحَٰبُ ٱلنَّارِ ۖ هُمْ فِيهَا خَٰلِدُونَ
- 58:18 يَوْمَ يَبْعَثُهُمُ ٱللَّهُ جَمِيعًۭا فَيَحْلِفُونَ لَهُۥ كَمَا يَحْلِفُونَ لَكُمْ ۖ وَيَحْسَبُونَ أَنَّهُمْ عَلَىٰ شَىْءٍ ۚ أَلَآ إِنَّهُمْ هُمُ ٱلْكَٰذِبُونَ
- 58:19 ٱسْتَحْوَذَ عَلَيْهِمُ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنُ فَأَنسَىٰهُمْ ذِكْرَ ٱللَّهِ ۚ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ حِزْبُ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنِ ۚ أَلَآ إِنَّ حِزْبَ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنِ هُمُ ٱلْخَٰسِرُونَ
- 58:20 إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يُحَآدُّونَ ٱللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُۥٓ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ فِى ٱلْأَذَلِّينَ
- 58:21 كَتَبَ ٱللَّهُ لَأَغْلِبَنَّ أَنَا۠ وَرُسُلِىٓ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ قَوِىٌّ عَزِيزٌۭ
- 58:22 لَّا تَجِدُ قَوْمًۭا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱللَّهِ وَٱلْيَوْمِ ٱلْءَاخِرِ يُوَآدُّونَ مَنْ حَآدَّ ٱللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُۥ وَلَوْ كَانُوٓا۟ ءَابَآءَهُمْ أَوْ أَبْنَآءَهُمْ أَوْ إِخْوَٰنَهُمْ أَوْ عَشِيرَتَهُمْ ۚ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ كَتَبَ فِى قُلُوبِهِمُ ٱلْإِيمَٰنَ وَأَيَّدَهُم بِرُوحٍۢ مِّنْهُ ۖ وَيُدْخِلُهُمْ جَنَّٰتٍۢ تَجْرِى مِن تَحْتِهَا ٱلْأَنْهَٰرُ خَٰلِدِينَ فِيهَا ۚ رَضِىَ ٱللَّهُ عَنْهُمْ وَرَضُوا۟ عَنْهُ ۚ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ حِزْبُ ٱللَّهِ ۚ أَلَآ إِنَّ حِزْبَ ٱللَّهِ هُمُ ٱلْمُفْلِحُونَ


===== _commentary/v16/work/s058/surah.r2/dictionary.md =====
# Dictionary: every branch of every focus root

Every root of the surah's words, each once, headed by the ayah and word where it first occurs; it also serves the later words listed with it.
One entry per branch: a Turkish label and Turkish glosses of its attested senses, then the classical dictionaries' own phrases with their source tags (ayn, sihah, tahdhib, maqayis, mufradat, jamhara). [kalıp] marks a sense the dictionaries attest only inside a fixed expression.
Identity roots come from the quran-data gateway. Word-scoped alternatives are cited analyses of this
exact word. **Echo roots** are observed but withheld mappings: sound-family candidates, not identity.

## س م ع (root_000741): 58:1 سَمِعَ, 58:1 يَسْمَعُ, 58:1 سَمِيعٌۢ

- **B001** duymak ve dikkatle dinlemek — sesi kulakla algılamak · işitme gücü veya duyma eylemi · duyma eylemi veya duyulan şey · dikkatle dinlemek · duymaya çalışarak kulak vermek · beni dinle ve söylediklerime kulak ver · dinle · söylenenleri çokça dinleyen kimse · işiten kimse · kendi kulağımla duydum; görmeyle ilgili aktarılmış yorum kaynakta reddedilir
  إيناس الشيء بالأذن (maqayis)؛ سمعت الشيء سمعا (maqayis;sihah;mufradat)؛ الاستماع الإصغاء (sihah;mufradat)؛ سماع أي اسمع (maqayis;sihah)؛ السمع سمع الإنسان وغيره (tahdhib)
- **B002** kulak ve kulak açıklığı — kulak veya işitme yeri · kulak veya kulak açıklığı · kulak açıklığı veya işitme yeri · kulak · iki kulak veya iki işitme yeri
  السمع الأذن وهي المسمعة (ayn;tahdhib)؛ المسمعة خرقها (ayn)؛ المسمع خرق الأذن (tahdhib;mufradat)؛ السامعة الأذن (sihah)؛ المسمعان الأذنان (sihah;tahdhib)
- **B003** anlayıp kabul etmek ve uymak — sözü anlayıp kabul etmek ve ona uymak
  تارة عن الفهم وتارة عن الطاعة (mufradat)؛ فهمنا وارتسمنا (mufradat)؛ فهمنا وهم لا يفهمون (mufradat)؛ لم يستعملوا هذه الحواس استعمالا يجدي عليهم (tahdhib)
- **B004** duyurmak veya kavratmak — duymasını sağlamak veya kavratmak · başkasına duyuran kimse
  سمعه الصوت وأسمعه (sihah)؛ السميع المسمع (sihah;tahdhib)؛ أسمعهم أي أفهمهم (mufradat)؛ فعلت ذلك تسمعتك وتسمعة لك أي لتسمعه (tahdhib)
- **B005** adı yayılıp tanınmak — yaymak, tanınır kılmak veya adını öne çıkarmak · güzel ün ve iyi ad · duyulup yayılan ve konuşulan şey · insanlar duysun diye yapılan gösteriş · insanların haberi birbirinden duyup yayması
  السمع الذكر الجميل (maqayis;sihah)؛ السماع ما سمعت به فشاع (ayn;tahdhib)؛ سمعت بالشيء إذا أشعته (maqayis)؛ فعله رياء وسمعة (ayn;sihah)؛ سمع به أي شهره (sihah)؛ سمعت بفلان في الناس إذا نوهت بذكره (tahdhib)
- **B006** kötü söz işittirip sövmek [kalıp] — sövmek ve hoşlanmayacağı sözleri yüzüne söylemek · sövmek veya duymaz olmasını dilemek
  أسمعه الحديث وسمعه أي شتمه (sihah)؛ أسمعت فلانا إذا سببته (mufradat)؛ أسمعك الله أي جعلك الله أصم (mufradat)؛ سمعت بالرجل تسميعا إذا نددت به وشهرته وفضحته (tahdhib)؛ أسمعته القبيح وشتمته (tahdhib)
- **B007** kulağa hoş gelen ezgili ses — şarkı veya kulağa hoş gelen güzel ses · kadın şarkıcı

  المبصرة المضيئة تبصرهم أي تجعلهم بصراء والمبصرة بالفتح الحجة (sihah)؛ مبصرة مضيئة وتبين لهم ومبصرا بها (tahdhib)؛ آياتنا مبصرة أي مضيئة للأبصار وقيل صار أهله بصراء وتبصرة أي تبصيرا وتبيانا (mufradat)
- **B004** kan izi — yerde veya bedende kan lekesi, kan izi · kanlar; kan bedelleri veya öçler
  البصيرة القطعة من الدم إذا وقعت بالأرض استدارت (maqayis;jamhara)؛ بصائر الدماء طرائقها على الجسد (ayn)؛ البصيرة من الدم ما كان على الأرض وشيء من الدم يستدل به على الرمية (sihah)؛ بصيرة من دم والجدية منها على الأرض والبصيرة الدية ومقدار الدرهم من الدم (tahdhib)؛ البصيرة قطعة من الدم تلمع (mufradat)

- **B006** kalın kenar ve ek yeri — bir şeyin yanı, kenarı veya kalın dış yüzü · iki deri parçasını birleştirip dikme · çadır, elbise veya kapta iki parça arası · iki parça arasında yamanmış ek
  بصر الشيء غلظه والبصر هو أن يضم أديم إلى أديم يخاطان والبصيرة ما بين شقتي البيت (maqayis)؛ البصر غلظ الشيء نحو بصر الجبل والسماء والحائط (ayn)؛ بصر كل شيء جلده الظاهر وثوب ذو بصر إذا كان غليظا وثيجا (jamhara)؛ البصر أن يضم أديم إلى أديم والبصر بالضم الجانب والحرف من كل شيء وغلظها (sihah)؛ الباصر الملفق بين شقتين والبصيرة الشقة التي تكون على الخباء والبصر أن يضم أديم إلى أديم (tahdhib)؛ البصر الناحية والبصيرة ما بين شقتي الثوب والمزادة وبصرت الثوب والأديم إذا خطت ذلك الموضع (mufradat)
- **B007** yumuşak parlak taş — yumuşak veya parlak taş; böyle taşlı yer · sonundaki ek düşmüş biçimiyle yumuşak taş · adı bu taşlı yer olan yere gitmek
  البصرة الحجارة الرخوة وبصر بكسر الباء من الأصل الثاني (maqayis)؛ البصرة أرض حجارتها جص والبصرة الحجارة التي فيها بعض اللين (ayn)؛ البصرة حجارة رخوة وبه سميت البصرة (jamhara)؛ البصرة حجارة رخوة إلى البياض وبصر بالكسر (sihah)؛ البصر والبصرة الحجارة البراقة وأرض كأنها جبل من جص وبصر الأرض غلظها وبصر فلان تبصيرا إذا أتى البصرة (tahdhib)؛ البصرة حجارة رخوة تلمع ويقال له بصر (mufradat)

  أم الرأس وهو الدماغ والشجة الآمة التي تبلغ أم الدماغ (maqayis)؛ أم الرأس وهو الدماغ ورجل مأموم والشجة الآمة التي تبلغ أم الدماغ (ayn)؛ أم رأسه بالعصا إذا أصاب أم رأسه وهي أم الدماغ (jamhara)؛ أم الدماع الجلدة التي تجمع الدماغ ويقال أيضا أم الرأس وأمه أي شجه آمة (sihah)؛ أمه شجه فحقيقته أن يصيب أم دماغه (mufradat)

  الأمة الدين (maqayis)؛ الأمة كل قوم في دينهم من أمتهم (ayn)؛ الأمة الملة (jamhara)؛ الأمة الطريقة والدين والإمة أيضا لغة في الأمة وهي الطريقة والدين (sihah)؛ إنا وجدنا آباءنا على أمة أي على دين مجتمع (mufradat)

# s058 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. P1 - Speech Placed Under Hearing and Account
- Semantic invariant: Spoken, secret, and inward discourse is brought under reception, disclosure, restraint, and consequence.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:1 anchors the heard complaint and dialogue, while 58:6-12 repeatedly expose, classify, restrain, or condition private and inward speech.
- Surprising reach: Private counsel also activates a flood-safe height, sin becomes delay from good, and charity physically goes ahead of a speaker as a purifying access-token.

#### Subchannel A. The Petition Enters a Fully Receptive Hearing
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A complaint becomes an interwoven verbal case whose utterance, reply, hearing, understanding, and sight are simultaneously present.
- Active motifs: auditory reception `quranic:root_000741:B001/m01`; heard understanding and response `quranic:root_000741:B003/m01`; articulated complaint `quranic:root_000814:B001/m01`; reply and verbal exchange `quranic:root_000369:B006/m01`; interwoven disputation `quranic:root_000229:B002/m01`; spoken utterance `quranic:root_001272:B001/m01`; visual perception `quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:1 `سَمِعَ/يَسْمَعُ/سَمِيعٌۢ`, `تَشْتَكِىٓ`, `تَحَاوُرَ`, `تُجَٰدِلُ`, `قَوْلَ`, `بَصِيرٌ`
- Synthesis: The woman's complaint is not a lone sound. It enters a scene of tightly engaged argument, answer, receptive hearing, understood speech, and sight. Hearing therefore carries both acoustic reception and responsive comprehension, making the opening petition a fully attended case rather than an unheard grievance.

#### Subchannel B. Secret Assembly Becomes an Exact Public Account
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The still are raised and gathered; private groupings are enumerated, accompanied, and made to yield a complete report of deeds that their agents forgot.
- Active motifs: stirring the still `quranic:root_000129:B001/m01`; gathering the dispersed `quranic:root_000259:B001/m01`; exhaustive enumeration `quranic:root_000332:B002/m01`; forgetting after knowledge `quranic:root_001501:B001/m01`; witnessed presence `quranic:root_000822:B001/m01`; three `quranic:root_000203:B001/m01`; four `quranic:root_000536:B001/m01`; five `quranic:root_000439:B001/m01`; six and the sixth `quranic:root_000689:B001/m01`; secret counsel `quranic:root_001476:B005/m01`; flood-safe high ground `quranic:root_001476:B004/m01`; disclosed knowledge `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`; delivered report `quranic:root_001464:B002/m01`; intentional deeds `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:6 `يَبْعَثُ`, `جَمِيعًا`, `أَحْصَىٰ`, `نَسُ`, `شَهِيدٌ`, `يُنَبِّئُ`, `عَمِلُ`; 58:7 `ثَلَٰثَةٍ`, `رَابِعُ`, `خَمْسَةٍ`, `سَادِسُ`, `نَّجْوَىٰ`, `يَعْلَمُ/عَلِيمٌ`, `يُنَبِّئُ`, `عَمِلُ`
- Synthesis: Resurrection supplies the gathering, enumeration, witness, and report. The number-pairs make every apparently closed group structurally open to an accompanying presence. Even the lexical image of `najwa` as high ground safe from a flood cannot furnish an escape: forgotten deeds are retained, counted, and returned as disclosed news.

#### Subchannel C. Prohibited Counsel Re-enters a Closed Loop
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A prohibition stops private counsel, but the participants turn back into the same secret exchange, now carrying delay from good, boundary-crossing, and disobedience.
- Active motifs: restraining prohibition `quranic:root_001560:B001/m01`; return after departure `quranic:root_001058:B001/m01`; secret counsel `quranic:root_001476:B005/m01`; delay from good `quranic:root_000013:B001/m01`; transgression beyond a limit `quranic:root_000993:B001/m01`; departure from obedience `quranic:root_001022:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:8 `نُهُ/نُهُ`, `يَعُودُ`, `نَّجْوَىٰ/يَتَنَٰجَ`, `إِثْمِ`, `عُدْوَٰنِ`, `مَعْصِيَتِ`
- Synthesis: The scene is iterative rather than static. Prohibition establishes a stopping point, return reopens the act, and the reopened secret is loaded with three distinct operations: lagging behind good, crossing a rightful boundary, and leaving commanded obedience.

#### Subchannel D. Counterfeit Greeting and Inner Challenge Reach Their Terminus
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An outward greeting invokes life while inward speech challenges punishment; the hidden claim is answered by a sufficient fire and terminal destination.
- Active motifs: life-invoking greeting `quranic:root_000383:B007/m01`; unvoiced inner saying `quranic:root_001272:B012/m01`; inward mind and intent `quranic:root_001533:B013/m01`; sufficiency `quranic:root_000318:B003/m01`; punitive pain `quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`; meeting the heat of fire `quranic:root_000880:B003/m01`; arrival at an outcome `quranic:root_000897:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:8 `حَيَّ/يُحَيِّ`, `يَقُولُ/نَقُولُ`, `أَنفُسِ`, `حَسْبُ`, `يُعَذِّبُ`, `يَصْلَ`, `مَصِيرُ`
- Synthesis: The social form of blessing is hollowed out by a concealed challenge. The channel joins surface politeness, unspoken calculation, and final consequence: a greeting that nominally asks for life is inverted into contact with fire, which is declared sufficient and becomes the destination.

#### Subchannel E. Counsel Is Recast as Protected Good
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Private exchange is redirected away from sin and aggression into expansive good, while piety supplies both an external barrier and an inward act of self-guarding.
- Active motifs: secret counsel `quranic:root_001476:B005/m01`; expansive good and obedience `quranic:root_000104:B002/m01`; protective barrier `quranic:root_001677:B001/m01`; self-placement in protection `quranic:root_001677:B002/m01`; gathering toward a destination `quranic:root_000324:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:9 `تَنَٰجَيْ/تَتَنَٰجَ/تَنَٰجَ`, `بِرِّ`, `تَّقْوَىٰ/ٱتَّقُ`, `تُحْشَرُ`
- Synthesis: The same private form is retained but its payload and safeguards change. Good replaces harmful content, while `taqwa` operates as both a barrier against injury and the act of placing oneself behind that barrier. The prospect of being driven into a final gathering keeps the protected exchange accountable.

#### Subchannel F. The Remote Whisper Can Roughen but Not Independently Injure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A rebellious, distancing source uses secret speech to roughen the believer's inner state, yet actual injury remains licensed and reliance is transferred to God.
- Active motifs: secret counsel `quranic:root_001476:B005/m01`; distance and severance `quranic:root_000796:B001/m01`; rebellious tempter `quranic:root_000796:B004/m01`; inner roughness from grief `quranic:root_000317:B001/m01`; harm as loss `quranic:root_000907:B001/m01`; permission and licensed entry `quranic:root_000022:B004/m01`; reliance `quranic:root_001681:B002/m01`; settled security of heart `quranic:root_000054:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:10 `نَّجْوَىٰ`, `شَّيْطَٰنِ`, `يَحْزُنَ`, `ضَآرِّ`, `إِذْنِ`, `يَتَوَكَّلِ`, `ءَامَنُ/مُؤْمِنُونَ`
- Synthesis: The whisper has emotional force but not autonomous causal force. Its distancing source can produce the rough texture of grief, while injury itself remains behind a permission-gate. Reliance relocates agency and restores the heart's secure stillness.

#### Subchannel G. A Gift Goes Ahead of Private Access
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A material gift is advanced before the private encounter, purifying the approach; inability is met by covering forgiveness and mercy.
- Active motifs: advance into the foreground `quranic:root_001207:B004/m01`; what stands before the hands `quranic:root_001693:B008/m01`; secret counsel `quranic:root_001476:B005/m01`; charitable transfer `quranic:root_000852:B006/m01`; beneficial good `quranic:root_000452:B001/m01`; moral purification `quranic:root_000953:B005/m01`; protective covering of fault `quranic:root_001096:B002/m01`; compassionate tenderness `quranic:root_000552:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:12 `قَدِّمُ`, `يَدَىْ`, `نَٰجَيْ/نَجْوَىٰ`, `صَدَقَةً`, `خَيْرٌ`, `أَطْهَرُ`, `غَفُورٌ`, `رَّحِيمٌ`
- Synthesis: Access to private speech receives a threshold mechanism: the gift moves ahead of the speaker and occupies the space "between the hands." It cleans the approach by turning privileged access into outward benefit, while failure of means is not confused with refusal and is covered by forgiveness and mercy.

### 2. P1 - Verbal Misclassification Repaired Through Embodied Sequence
- Semantic invariant: A speech-act that distorts an embodied relation is repaired through bodily release, delayed contact, timed restraint, and provision.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:1-4 moves from dispute and zihar through maternal and marital classification to release, waiting before touch, fasting, and feeding.
- Surprising reach: The dispute tightens like plaited cord, the false category turns on the body's back, and repair answers by unbinding a neck and imposing an ordered bodily-social sequence.

#### Subchannel A. Twisted Speech Cannot Manufacture Maternity
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Tightly wound argument turns a spouse's back into a maternal comparison, but false saying and semantic deviation fail against birth as the constitutive maternal event.
- Active motifs: tightly plaited cord `quranic:root_000229:B001/m01`; zihar formula `quranic:root_000970:B010/m01`; back opposed to belly `quranic:root_000970:B002/m01`; marital spouse `quranic:root_000652:B002/m01`; mother as bearer and nurturer `quranic:root_000053:B001/m01`; childbirth `quranic:root_001683:B003/m01`; fabricated attribution `quranic:root_001272:B005/m01`; reprehensible act `quranic:root_001550:B006/m01`; deviation from the straight course `quranic:root_000654:B001/m01`; falsehood `quranic:root_000654:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:1 `تُجَٰدِلُ`, `زَوْجِ`; 58:2 `يُظَٰهِرُ`, `أُمَّهَٰتِ/أُمَّهَٰتُ`, `وَلَدْ`, `يَقُولُ/قَوْلِ`, `مُنكَرًا`, `زُورًا`
- Synthesis: The lexical scene makes the verbal distortion tactile: argument is a tightened plait and zihar hinges on the back. Yet the utterance cannot rebuild kinship. Maternity remains grounded in bearing and birth, so the spoken substitution is exposed as both a deviation and a fabricated attribution.

#### Subchannel B. Release Reopens the Threshold of Marital Contact
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Return after the utterance requires a bound person to be released before spouses may resume bodily contact.
- Active motifs: return after departure `quranic:root_001058:B001/m01`; freedom from bondage `quranic:root_000306:B002/m01`; neck standing for the bound person `quranic:root_000584:B004/m01`; temporal precedence `quranic:root_001198:B002/m01`; marital touching `quranic:root_001423:B002/m01`; heart-softening admonition `quranic:root_001663:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:3 `يَعُودُ`, `تَحْرِيرُ`, `رَقَبَةٍ`, `قَبْلِ`, `يَتَمَآسَّ`, `تُوعَظُ`
- Synthesis: The repair is a release mechanism, not a compensating statement. A neck marked by restraint is freed, and that liberation must precede renewed contact. Admonition supplies the moral pressure that joins bodily emancipation to restoration of the marital threshold.

#### Subchannel C. Timed Restraint Converts Failure of Means into Provision
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: When release is unavailable, the repair becomes two consecutive months of bodily abstention; incapacity redirects it again into feeding sixty people in need, all within a marked boundary.
- Active motifs: fasting as restraint `quranic:root_000894:B001/m01`; lunar month `quranic:root_000823:B001/m01`; uninterrupted succession `quranic:root_000175:B004/m01`; capacity to act `quranic:root_000956:B003/m01`; feeding another `quranic:root_000934:B002/m01`; the number sixty `quranic:root_000673:B001/m01`; poverty and need `quranic:root_000726:B006/m01`; discriminating limit `quranic:root_000002:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:4 `صِيَامُ`, `شَهْرَيْنِ`, `مُتَتَابِعَيْنِ`, `يَسْتَطِعْ`, `إِطْعَامُ`, `سِتِّينَ`, `مِسْكِينًا`, `حُدُودُ`
- Synthesis: The alternatives preserve rigor while changing material. Personal release becomes prolonged self-restraint, then social nourishment. Consecutive time and an exact beneficiary count give the process measurable form, while the boundary prevents the graded alternatives from becoming arbitrary substitutions.

### 3. P1 - Boundary Opposition Is Forced into Reversal
- Semantic invariant: Deliberate opposition to a marked limit is answered by disclosure, downward movement, defeat, and humiliation.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:4-5 names divine limits, opposition to God and the messenger, clear signs, abasement, and humiliating punishment.
- Surprising reach: The adversary who takes a position across the boundary is not merely defeated but spatially inverted, driven back and cast onto the face as signs descend into visibility.

#### Subchannel A. Opposition Takes Position Across a Marked Edge
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A discriminating boundary defines the lawful field; active opposition places itself against that edge and against the bearer of the message while concealing the truth it carries.
- Active motifs: discriminating boundary `quranic:root_000002:B001/m01`; active opposition `quranic:root_000002:B003/m01`; messenger and carried message `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`; concealment and denial of truth `quranic:root_001307:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:4 `حُدُودُ`, `رَسُولِ`, `كَٰفِرِينَ`; 58:5 `يُحَآدُّ`, `رَسُولَ`, `كَٰفِرِينَ`
- Synthesis: The same root makes limit and opposition mutually legible: the limit marks an edge, while opposition is a stance taken against it. Denial adds a covering operation, and the messenger carries the speech whose authority that covering attempts to obstruct.

#### Subchannel B. Descending Signs Expose and Overturn the Opponent
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Signs are delivered downward and made manifest; the resisting party is repulsed, cast face-down, and assigned painful abasement.
- Active motifs: repulse and abasement `quranic:root_001279:B001/m01`; casting onto the face `quranic:root_001279:B002/m01`; delivery from above `quranic:root_001492:B002/m01`; visible sign `quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`; disclosure into visibility `quranic:root_000170:B004/m01`; semantic clarification `quranic:root_000170:B005/m01`; punitive pain `quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`; humiliating diminution `quranic:root_001608:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:5 `كُبِتُ/كُبِتَ`, `أَنزَلْ`, `ءَايَٰتٍۭ`, `بَيِّنَٰتٍ`, `عَذَابٌ`, `مُّهِينٌ`
- Synthesis: Revelation and defeat move in opposite vertical directions. The signs descend but become evident and clarifying; the opponent is driven downward onto the face. What attempted to cover the message is answered by disclosure, and resistant elevation ends in embodied humiliation.

### 4. P1 - Assembly Space Opens and Then Rises
- Semantic invariant: Commanded bodily movement reorganizes communal space horizontally and maps that reorganization into vertical, graded status.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:11 commands making room, sitting, rising, and identifies raised degrees for believers and people of knowledge.
- Surprising reach: A seating instruction becomes a spatial mechanism: horizontal expansion prevents exclusion, while rising from the seat is converted into stepwise epistemic elevation.

#### Subchannel A. Making Room Produces Shared Habitable Space
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Seated bodies receive an uttered instruction to widen the assembly, and repeated acts of opening produce room for others.
- Active motifs: opening spaciousness `quranic:root_001153:B001/m01`; settled sitting `quranic:root_000254:B001/m01`; spoken instruction `quranic:root_001272:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:11 `تَفَسَّحُ/ٱفْسَحُ/يَفْسَحِ`, `مَجَٰلِسِ`, `قِيلَ/قِيلَ`
- Synthesis: The scene is an ergonomic redistribution of bodies. Speech initiates the act, settled occupants yield space, and the repeated root of widening makes divine opening answer human opening without changing the operation.

#### Subchannel B. Rising Is Translated into Graded Elevation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Bodily departure from the seat activates terrain-height and upward movement, which are then converted into rank acquired degree by degree through faith and knowledge.
- Active motifs: raised terrain `quranic:root_001504:B001/m01`; rising from a seat `quranic:root_001504:B002/m01`; lifting a body `quranic:root_000582:B001/m01`; elevation of status `quranic:root_000582:B002/m01`; stepwise degrees `quranic:root_000468:B003/m01`; disclosed knowledge `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`; heart-set faith `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; knowledge of inward realities `quranic:root_000387:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:11 `ٱنشُزُ/ٱنشُزُ`, `يَرْفَعِ`, `دَرَجَٰتٍ`, `عِلْمَ`, `ءَامَنُ/ءَامَنُ`, `خَبِيرٌ`
- Synthesis: One root links leaving the assembly to a physical rise, and another separates lifting from honor. The result is a vertical social map in which faith and knowledge occupy degrees rather than crowding a single level, under knowledge that reaches the inward state as well as the visible posture.

### 5. P2 - Withheld Advance Is Redirected into Standing Duties
- Semantic invariant: Failure at a one-time threshold payment is met by return and the re-establishment of durable bodily, economic, and obedient practices.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:13 names fear of advancing charity before private counsel, failure to do so, divine turning, prayer, alms, and obedience.
- Surprising reach: A feared advance-payment does not close access permanently; the scene pivots from withheld movement to upright posture, recurring gift, growth, purification, and guided compliance.

#### Subchannel A. Fear Arrests the Advance at the Threshold
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Fear mixed with care, and possibly withholding, stops a charitable gift from moving ahead of private counsel; nonperformance is answered by an opening for return.
- Active motifs: anxious solicitude `quranic:root_000803:B003/m01`; withholding through stinginess `quranic:root_000803:B004/m01`; advance into the foreground `quranic:root_001207:B004/m01`; what stands before the hands `quranic:root_001693:B008/m01`; secret counsel `quranic:root_001476:B005/m01`; charitable transfer `quranic:root_000852:B006/m01`; performed act `quranic:root_001167:B001/m01`; opened return through repentance `quranic:root_000189:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:13 `أَشْفَقْ`, `تُقَدِّمُ`, `يَدَىْ`, `نَجْوَىٰ`, `صَدَقَٰتٍ`, `تَفْعَلُ`, `تَابَ`
- Synthesis: The scene stalls at the front edge of the encounter: concern and withholding prevent the gift from going ahead. The failure is named as an unrealized act, but divine turning reopens movement rather than fixing the participants at the failed threshold.

#### Subchannel B. Standing, Giving, and Obeying Replace the Failed Gate
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The reopened path is stabilized through upright prayer, transfer of alms, growth and purification, and compliant movement under the messenger's instruction.
- Active motifs: upright bodily standing `quranic:root_001273:B002/m01`; prescribed prayer `quranic:root_000879:B003/m01`; giving and delivery `quranic:root_000009:B002/m01`; growth and increase `quranic:root_000637:B001/m01`; purity and rectification `quranic:root_000637:B002/m01`; obedient compliance `quranic:root_000956:B001/m01`; messenger and carried message `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`; inward knowledge `quranic:root_000387:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:13 `أَقِيمُ`, `صَّلَوٰةَ`, `ءَاتُ`, `زَّكَوٰةَ`, `أَطِيعُ`, `رَسُولَ`, `خَبِيرٌۢ`
- Synthesis: A single access-payment gives way to durable practices. Prayer erects the body, alms circulate value and activate growth-purity, and obedience aligns motion with instruction. The shift turns a missed transaction into an ongoing disciplined economy known from within.

### 6. P2 - Oath-Built Shields Become Failed Defenses
- Semantic invariant: False verbal and material protections are constructed, used to obstruct a path, and then exposed as unable to prevent judgment.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:14-18 presents false alliance and oath, oaths taken as a shield, obstruction of God's path, failed wealth and offspring, fire-companionship, and renewed oaths after resurrection.
- Surprising reach: The oath becomes literal defensive equipment and a mountain-like roadblock on a route to water; when that cover fails, wealth and children also fail as insulation and fire becomes the enduring companion.

#### Subchannel A. A False Oath Fastens a Liminal Alliance
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Affiliation with an angered group is verbally fastened by an oath that also carries the sense of covenant and persistent attachment, although the speakers know it is false.
- Active motifs: alliance and protective friendship `quranic:root_001684:B004/m01`; social group `quranic:root_001273:B001/m01`; wrath seeking requital `quranic:root_001092:B001/m01`; sworn oath `quranic:root_000349:B001/m01`; covenant and attachment `quranic:root_000349:B002/m01`; falsehood `quranic:root_001290:B001/m01`; disclosed knowledge `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:14 `تَوَلَّ`, `قَوْمًا`, `غَضِبَ`, `يَحْلِفُ`, `كَذِبِ`, `يَعْلَمُ`
- Synthesis: The oath is not only a statement but an attempted fastening of affiliation. Its covenantal sense tries to stabilize people who occupy an in-between social position, yet knowledge and falsehood coexist in the same scene, making the verbal bond knowingly counterfeit.

#### Subchannel B. The Oath Becomes a Shield and a Blocked Water-Road
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Oaths are acquired as protective equipment; concealment hardens into a shield, which turns into obstruction of a traversable path and, lexically, a barrier across access to water.
- Active motifs: acquiring a device `quranic:root_000018:B010/m01`; oath `quranic:root_001698:B003/m01`; concealment `quranic:root_000266:B001/m01`; protective shield `quranic:root_000266:B008/m01`; active obstruction `quranic:root_000848:B001/m01`; road to water `quranic:root_000848:B004/m01`; mountain barrier `quranic:root_000848:B005/m01`; traversable road `quranic:root_000672:B001/m01`; humiliating diminution `quranic:root_001608:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:16 `ٱتَّخَذُ`, `أَيْمَٰنَ`, `جُنَّةً`, `صَدُّ`, `سَبِيلِ`, `مُّهِينٌ`
- Synthesis: The verse's own shield metaphor is extended by the branch assembly. The acquired oath hides its user, wards off exposure, and blocks passage; the obstruction additionally evokes a mountain cutting off a route to water. The apparent defensive height ends not in safety but in humiliation.

#### Subchannel C. Wealth and Offspring Cannot Purchase Exit from Fire-Companionship
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Accumulated property and descendants are tested as sources of sufficiency, fail to substitute for protection, and are replaced by permanent companionship with fire.
- Active motifs: adequacy and substitution `quranic:root_001110:B002/m01`; accumulated wealth `quranic:root_001457:B001/m01`; offspring `quranic:root_001683:B001/m01`; knowable entity `quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`; companionship and attachment `quranic:root_000844:B001/m01`; blazing fire `quranic:root_001564:B002/m01`; fixed continuance `quranic:root_000429:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:17 `تُغْنِىَ`, `أَمْوَٰلُ`, `أَوْلَٰدُ`, `شَيْـًٔا`, `أَصْحَٰبُ`, `نَّارِ`, `خَٰلِدُونَ`
- Synthesis: Household assets are evaluated as substitutes and found unable to do the work of defense. The social relation then reverses: possessions and children do not accompany their owners out, while fire becomes their named companion and permanence fixes that attachment.

#### Subchannel D. The Oath Reflex Survives Resurrection but Not Disclosure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The still are raised and gathered, yet the same oath-performance is repeated before God; supposition sustains it briefly before falsehood is named.
- Active motifs: stirring the still `quranic:root_000129:B001/m01`; gathering the dispersed `quranic:root_000259:B001/m01`; sworn oath `quranic:root_000349:B001/m01`; supposition `quranic:root_000318:B002/m01`; knowable entity `quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`; falsehood `quranic:root_001290:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:18 `يَبْعَثُ`, `جَمِيعًا`, `يَحْلِفُ/يَحْلِفُ`, `يَحْسَبُ`, `شَىْءٍ`, `كَٰذِبُونَ`
- Synthesis: Resurrection changes the audience but not the practiced defense. The oath is reenacted as if it still establishes standing, and supposition supplies the imagined support. Naming the speakers as liars removes the final verbal cover.

### 7. P2 - Captured Memory Produces Rival Belonging
- Semantic invariant: Collective identity is formed by what governs memory, affection, support, and the boundary between ally and opponent.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:19 describes satanic seizure, induced forgetting, faction, and loss, while 58:22 subordinates affection and kinship to faith and opposition before naming God's party.
- Surprising reach: Satanic influence is violent herding and compressive pressure that ends in commercial loss; the counter-community is not inherited automatically from household architecture but formed by disciplined affection and remembered allegiance.

#### Subchannel A. Violent Seizure Drives Forgetting, Faction, and Loss
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A rebellious power seizes and drives its subjects, removes remembered mention, compresses them into a faction, and delivers both general diminution and a failed bargain.
- Active motifs: violent driving `quranic:root_000368:B001/m01`; seizure and domination `quranic:root_000368:B003/m01`; distance and severance `quranic:root_000796:B001/m01`; rebellious tempter `quranic:root_000796:B004/m01`; induced forgetting `quranic:root_001501:B001/m01`; remembered presence `quranic:root_000516:B003/m01`; spoken remembrance `quranic:root_000516:B004/m01`; faction on one opinion `quranic:root_000315:B001/m01`; compressive pressure `quranic:root_000315:B003/m01`; general loss `quranic:root_000409:B001/m01`; commercial loss `quranic:root_000409:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:19 `ٱسْتَحْوَذَ`, `شَّيْطَٰنُ/شَّيْطَٰنِ/شَّيْطَٰنِ`, `أَنسَىٰ`, `ذِكْرَ`, `حِزْبُ/حِزْبَ`, `خَٰسِرُونَ`
- Synthesis: Control is rendered as a kinetic capture: subjects are driven and gathered under domination. The key operation is mnemonic dispossession, removing both inward recollection and outward mention. The resulting party is a pressured bloc whose cohesion yields loss rather than strength, including the image of a ruined transaction.

#### Subchannel B. Faith Redraws Affection Across the Kinship Structure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Faith establishes a boundary at which affection toward active opponents is refused even when the relation is fatherhood, descent, siblinghood, or clan.
- Active motifs: social group `quranic:root_001273:B001/m01`; heart-set faith `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; affection `quranic:root_001634:B001/m01`; active opposition `quranic:root_000002:B003/m01`; fatherhood and nurture `quranic:root_000007:B001/m01`; descent and filiation `quranic:root_000156:B007/m01`; sibling bond `quranic:root_000020:B001/m01`; clan and shared affair `quranic:root_001016:B013/m01`; messenger and carried message `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:22 `قَوْمًا`, `يُؤْمِنُ/إِيمَٰنَ`, `يُوَآدُّ`, `حَآدَّ`, `ءَابَآءَ`, `أَبْنَآءَ`, `إِخْوَٰنَ`, `عَشِيرَتَ`, `رَسُولَ`
- Synthesis: The scene tests four concentric kin relations and denies that inherited proximity alone determines loyalty. Faith does not erase father, child, sibling, or clan as relations; it redraws the direction of affection when those relations become active participants in opposition to the message.

#### Subchannel C. Mutual Satisfaction Stabilizes the Counter-Party
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The faithful are named as a party held together by shared conviction, reciprocal satisfaction, and an outcome of durable success.
- Active motifs: heart-set faith `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; faction on one opinion `quranic:root_000315:B001/m01`; reciprocal satisfaction `quranic:root_000569:B003/m01`; victorious continuance `quranic:root_001175:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:22 `يُؤْمِنُ/إِيمَٰنَ`, `حِزْبُ/حِزْبَ`, `رَضِىَ/رَضُ`, `مُفْلِحُونَ`
- Synthesis: In contrast to a party produced by seizure and forgetting, this party is internally affirmed and relationally reciprocal. Mutual satisfaction supplies the social bond, while `falah` gives the group an outcome that combines attainment with continued good.

### 8. P2 - Fixed Decree Assembles Conquering Force
- Semantic invariant: Opposition at a boundary is answered by an already fixed judgment and strength gathered into an overcoming unity.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:20-21 moves from opposition and lowest rank to a written decree of victory, divine strength, and overpowering might.
- Surprising reach: Strength is not diffuse energy but strands gathered like a rope, while writing fixes the outcome before the contest and turns attempted resistance into a vertical role reversal.

#### Subchannel A. Boundary Opposition Descends into the Lowest Rank
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Those who position themselves against the marked edge and its messenger are broken under domination and placed low.
- Active motifs: discriminating boundary `quranic:root_000002:B001/m01`; active opposition `quranic:root_000002:B003/m01`; breaking under domination `quranic:root_000519:B001/m01`; lowered and hanging position `quranic:root_000519:B005/m01`; messenger and carried message `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:20 `يُحَآدُّ`, `رَسُولَ`, `أَذَلِّينَ`
- Synthesis: Opposition is again spatial: it takes a stance at the boundary. Its outcome is equally spatial but reversed, combining loss of resistance with low placement. The attempted counter-position therefore becomes a location beneath the order it opposed.

#### Subchannel B. Written Outcome and Braided Strength Converge in Victory
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A decree fixes victory; dispatch, rope-like gathered strength, restored might, and coercive conquest then materialize it.
- Active motifs: fixed decree `quranic:root_001283:B003/m01`; overpowering conquest `quranic:root_001098:B001/m01`; strength gathered like rope strands `quranic:root_001274:B001/m01`; might opposed to abasement `quranic:root_001008:B001/m01`; coercive victory `quranic:root_001008:B002/m01`; dispatch and release `quranic:root_000563:B001/m01`; messenger and carried message `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:21 `كَتَبَ`, `أَغْلِبَ`, `قَوِىٌّ`, `عَزِيزٌ`, `رُسُلِ`
- Synthesis: Writing establishes the judgment as fixed rather than improvised. The force that executes it is internally assembled like the strands of a strong rope, joined to dispatch and overpowering victory. Might answers the preceding abasement by reserving durable strength for God and the messengers.

### 9. P2 - Inward Inscription Opens into Irrigated Permanence
- Semantic invariant: Durable belonging is established first as an inscribed, strengthened interior and then as entry into a sheltered, flowing, cultivated habitat.
- Surface relation: direct; 58:22 writes faith in hearts, reinforces it with spirit, admits the faithful to gardens with rivers below, fixes their residence, and names satisfaction and success.
- Surprising reach: Faith becomes text lodged in the heart's core and braced by life-force; outwardly, success recalls splitting and farming earth as rivers cut channels through a tree-covered enclosure.

#### Subchannel A. Faith Is Written into the Heart and Reinforced with Life-Force
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Faith is organized as writing and binding decree inside the heart's core, registers its bearers in a group, and is reinforced by a life-giving and force-bearing spirit.
- Active motifs: written text `quranic:root_001283:B002/m01`; binding heart-inscription `quranic:root_001283:B003/m02`; registration in a company `quranic:root_001283:B004/m01`; heart and understanding `quranic:root_001248:B001/m01`; innermost core `quranic:root_001248:B002/m01`; heart-set faith `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; strengthening support `quranic:root_000071:B001/m01`; life-bearing spirit `quranic:root_000609:B001/m01`; force-bearing wind `quranic:root_000609:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:22 `كَتَبَ`, `قُلُوبِ`, `يُؤْمِنُ/إِيمَٰنَ`, `أَيَّدَ`, `رُوحٍ`
- Synthesis: The interior is treated as a durable writing surface rather than a passing emotional state. Faith is written into the heart and its core, the inscription also registers a corporate identity, and spirit supplies both animation and reinforcing force. Interior conviction thus becomes marked, supported structure.

#### Subchannel B. Entry Leads to a Sheltered, Flowing, Cultivated Abode
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The marked group enters tree-covered gardens where rivers cut and flow below; fixed residence, reciprocal satisfaction, cultivation, and success complete the habitat.
- Active motifs: entry into an interior `quranic:root_000464:B001/m01`; garden sheltered by trees `quranic:root_000266:B003/m01`; flowing current `quranic:root_000240:B001/m01`; lower spatial level `quranic:root_000177:B001/m01`; river cutting the ground `quranic:root_001559:B001/m01`; fixed continuance `quranic:root_000429:B001/m01`; reciprocal satisfaction `quranic:root_000569:B003/m01`; splitting open the earth `quranic:root_001175:B001/m01`; cultivating farmer `quranic:root_001175:B003/m01`; victorious continuance `quranic:root_001175:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:22 `يُدْخِلُ`, `جَنَّٰتٍ`, `تَجْرِى`, `تَحْتِ`, `أَنْهَٰرُ`, `خَٰلِدِينَ`, `رَضِىَ/رَضُ`, `مُفْلِحُونَ`
- Synthesis: The outward result matches the durability of the inward inscription. Entry passes into a protected garden, but this enclosure is not stagnant: water actively cuts channels and flows beneath it. The lexical reach of `falah` adds opened and cultivated earth to final success, making permanence a living irrigated settlement rather than mere immobility.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. P1 - Discourse as Channel, Reservoir, and Purifying Water
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A small stream is directed through a watercourse into a holding basin and deep reservoir, where it sustains life and supplies purification.
- Active motifs: small river-channel `quranic:root_000229:B005/m01`; water collected in a hollow `quranic:root_000281:B003/m01`; deep gathered water `quranic:root_001040:B005/m01`; directed watercourse `quranic:root_000009:B004/m01`; life-sustaining water `quranic:root_001533:B008/m01`; water that purifies another `quranic:root_000953:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:1 `تُجَٰدِلُ`; 58:7 `يَعْلَمُ/عَلِيمٌ`; 58:8 `جَآءُ`, `أَنفُسِ`; 58:11 `أُوتُ`, `عِلْمَ`; 58:12 `أَطْهَرُ`
- Synthesis: The lexical hydrology gives P1's discourse-management a concrete mechanism. Argument supplies a narrow stream, coming and inwardness supply a collecting hollow and life-water, knowledge becomes a deep reservoir, granted capacity directs a channel, and purification names the final use of the flow. The scene reframes hearing, counsel, opened assembly, and conditioned access as the difference between water responsibly channelled and water merely pooled in secrecy.

### S2. Whole Surah - A Hide Worked into a Shield
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Hair-on hide is stripped, treated with measured tanning material, hardened or folded, and sewn edge-to-edge into a shield.
- Active motifs: hair-on raw hide `quranic:root_000844:B006/m01`; scraping and stripping hide `quranic:root_001476:B003/m01`; measured dose of tanning material `quranic:root_001533:B007/m01`; tanned and cracked hide `quranic:root_000369:B004/m01`; hard or folded camel hide used as a shield `quranic:root_001092:B008/m01`; sewing one hide edge to another `quranic:root_000121:B006/m01`; stitching hide pieces together `quranic:root_001283:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:1 `تَحَاوُرَ`, `بَصِيرٌ`; 58:7 `نَّجْوَىٰ`; 58:8 `أَنفُسِ`; 58:14 `غَضِبَ`; 58:17 `أَصْحَٰبُ`; 58:21 `كَتَبَ`
- Synthesis: Across the surah's speech and judgment vocabulary, raw hide is stripped, dosed, hardened, folded, and stitched into protection. This gives the oath-shield of 58:16 a manufacturing history: verbal defense is a deliberately fabricated artifact rather than an inherent refuge. The craft scene materializes how oaths can be assembled to obstruct the path while pressing the surface image further, since laborious construction still cannot make the shield effective against judgment.

### S3. Whole Surah - The Marked Road and Beacon
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A traveler proceeds along a clear, frequented road through a junction while standards, landmarks, a visible beacon, and a public call keep the route legible.
- Active motifs: clear road over easy ground `quranic:root_001464:B007/m01`; traveled road and junction `quranic:root_000009:B010/m01`; walking and proceeding along a route `quranic:root_000468:B001/m01`; raised visible standard `quranic:root_000531:B011/m01`; distinguishing road landmark `quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`; visible route marker and beacon `quranic:root_001564:B005/m01`; public call and its audible reach `quranic:root_000022:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:6 `يُنَبِّئُ`; 58:7 `تَرَ`, `يَعْلَمُ/عَلِيمٌ`; 58:10 `إِذْنِ`; 58:11 `أُوتُ`, `دَرَجَٰتٍ`; 58:17 `نَّارِ`
- Synthesis: The route, its movement, and its signaling apparatus form one public wayfinding system. This materializes the path of God in 58:16 as a traversable way made legible by report, knowledge, visible standards, boundary marks, beacon, and summons. Secret counsel and oath-shields are thereby reframed not only as false speech but as practices of obstruction and misdirection carried out despite an openly marked route.

### S4. Whole Surah - From Covered Seed to a Shared Meal
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A farmer covers seed, tends its growth into ears of grain, carries the harvest to the threshing floor, and turns wheat flour into a cooked meal for a person in need.
- Active motifs: farmer covering seed with soil `quranic:root_001307:B008/m01`; crop growth and yield `quranic:root_000009:B007/m01`; thickening and flowering vegetation `quranic:root_000266:B011/m01`; emerging ear of grain `quranic:root_000672:B008/m01`; harvested crop carried to the threshing floor `quranic:root_000582:B006/m01`; wheat and grain food `quranic:root_000104:B006/m01`; flour-and-milk porridge `quranic:root_000306:B012/m01`; feeding a person in need `quranic:root_000934:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:3 `تَحْرِيرُ`; 58:4 `إِطْعَامُ`, `كَٰفِرِينَ`; 58:9 `بِرِّ`; 58:11 `أُوتُ`, `يَرْفَعِ`; 58:16 `سَبِيلِ`; 58:22 `جَنَّٰتٍ`
- Synthesis: The sequence gives expiatory feeding in 58:4 its upstream labor: hidden seed becomes growth, ear, harvest, grain, cooked food, and finally nourishment for another person. It materializes the surah's expiatory feeding and pre-consultation charity as a provisioning economy rather than isolated transfers, and it joins those duties to the cultivated garden of 58:22. The primary demand is thus reframed as disciplined allegiance whose yield must become usable care.

### S5. P1 to P2 - Account Becomes Record and Enrollment
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (58:6-7) retains forgotten deeds as an enumerated, delivered account; P2 (58:19, 21-22) moves from suppressed remembrance to documentary fixation, judgment, and enrollment. The bridge is a causal role progression from count to report to authoritative record.
- Scene or process: An account is exhaustively counted and reported, converted into a document and binding written judgment, and completed by entering a name in an official register or company.
- Active motifs: forgetting after prior retention `quranic:root_001501:B001/m01`; exhaustive enumeration `quranic:root_000332:B002/m01`; delivered report `quranic:root_001464:B002/m01`; document recording a right `quranic:root_000516:B008/m01`; written sheet `quranic:root_001283:B002/m01`; binding written judgment `quranic:root_001283:B003/m01`; name entered in a register or company `quranic:root_001283:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:6 `أَحْصَىٰ`, `نَسُ`, `يُنَبِّئُ`; 58:7 `يُنَبِّئُ`; 58:19 `أَنسَىٰ`, `ذِكْرَ`; 58:21 `كَتَبَ`; 58:22 `كَتَبَ`
- Synthesis: P1 establishes that failure of personal memory does not erase action: enumeration and report retain what agents forget. P2 shows that remembrance can be actively suppressed, then shifts authority from recollection to record: a right has documentary form, judgment is fixed in writing, and a name can be entered in a company. The bridge materializes accountability and allegiance as one administrative sequence, so forgotten conduct remains adjudicable while durable belonging is established by the record that governs it.

### S6. P1 and P2 - Kinship Grounds Status but Not Allegiance
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (58:1-4) grounds maternity in bearing and birth against a fabricated verbal classification; P2 (58:22) acknowledges father, child, sibling, and clan while denying that those ties command affection toward active opposition. The bridge is a reversal in the normative force of embodied kinship.
- Scene or process: A false utterance fails to manufacture a bodily family status, while genuine inherited relations remain real but lose authority to determine moral allegiance.
- Active motifs: zihar formula `quranic:root_000970:B010/m01`; mother as bearer and nurturer `quranic:root_000053:B001/m01`; childbirth `quranic:root_001683:B003/m01`; fabricated attribution `quranic:root_001272:B005/m01`; fatherhood and nurture `quranic:root_000007:B001/m01`; descent and filiation `quranic:root_000156:B007/m01`; sibling bond `quranic:root_000020:B001/m01`; clan and shared affair `quranic:root_001016:B013/m01`; affection `quranic:root_001634:B001/m01`; active opposition `quranic:root_000002:B003/m01`; heart-set faith `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:2 `يُظَٰهِرُ`, `أُمَّهَٰتِ/أُمَّهَٰتُ`, `وَلَدْ`, `يَقُولُ/قَوْلِ`; 58:22 `ءَابَآءَ`, `أَبْنَآءَ`, `إِخْوَٰنَ`, `عَشِيرَتَ`, `يُوَآدُّ`, `حَآدَّ`, `يُؤْمِنُ/إِيمَٰنَ`
- Synthesis: P1 gives embodied kinship evidentiary force: a spoken analogy cannot turn a spouse into a mother because maternity is constituted by bearing and birth. P2 then denies embodied kinship automatic normative force: fatherhood, descent, siblinghood, and clan remain actual relations, but they cannot require affection toward active opposition. The bridge is therefore a deliberate reversal rather than a general family theme: the body fixes what the relation is, while allegiance determines what that relation may command.

### S7. P1 to P2 - From Distant Tether to Forced Herding
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (58:10) presents a distant rebellious whisper that roughens the inner state; P2 (58:19) presents seizure, induced forgetting, and faction. The bridge is a role progression from remote affective pull to the bodily control and collective steering of a group.
- Scene or process: A long twisted tether exerts a distant pull; a driving staff, violent herding, seizure, and gathering then turn that pull into directed collective movement.
- Active motifs: distance and severance `quranic:root_000796:B001/m01`; rebellious tempter `quranic:root_000796:B004/m01`; long tightly twisted tether `quranic:root_000796:B002/m01`; inner roughness from grief `quranic:root_000317:B001/m01`; violent driving of herd animals `quranic:root_000368:B001/m01`; seizure, domination, and gathering `quranic:root_000368:B003/m01`; induced forgetting `quranic:root_001501:B001/m01`; staff used to drive an animal `quranic:root_001501:B006/m01`; faction gathered on one opinion `quranic:root_000315:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:10 `شَّيْطَٰنِ`, `يَحْزُنَ`; 58:19 `ٱسْتَحْوَذَ`, `شَّيْطَٰنُ/شَّيْطَٰنِ`, `أَنسَىٰ`, `حِزْبُ/حِزْبَ`
- Synthesis: The same adversarial role changes scale across the pericopes. In P1 the whisper acts at a distance: the rope image makes private suggestion a pull, while its immediate reach is the roughening of grief and it lacks independent power. P2 completes the handling scene with a goad, violent driving, seizure, and gathering; induced forgetting redirects people into faction. The bridge materializes a progression from attempted affective tug to collective control, while P1's reliance marks the point at which the tether need not become a drive.

### S8. P1 to P2 - A Claim Proceeds from Covenant to Restitution
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (58:1-12) opens a litigated utterance, imposes repair, and retains deeds for report; P2 (58:13-22) resumes duty, liability, judgment, and fixed outcome. The bridge is a causal legal sequence from negotiated undertaking through transaction and actionable claim to restitution or waived penalty.
- Scene or process: Parties negotiate an affair and exchange an undertaking; their dealing generates a claim that is pursued, answered through accountability, and resolved by returning the right or waiving punishment.
- Active motifs: negotiated affair `quranic:root_001272:B009/m01`; reciprocal undertaking `quranic:root_000055:B005/m01`; transaction between people `quranic:root_001046:B005/m01`; claimant pursuing a right `quranic:root_000175:B005/m01`; accountability for wrongdoing `quranic:root_000018:B002/m01`; right returned to its owner `quranic:root_000609:B006/m01`; punishment waived and fault erased `quranic:root_001032:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:1 `قَوْلَ`; 58:2 `يَقُولُ/قَوْلِ`, `عَفُوٌّ`; 58:3 `قَالُ`, `تَعْمَلُ`; 58:4 `مُتَتَابِعَيْنِ`; 58:6 `عَمِلُ`; 58:7 `عَمِلُ`; 58:8 `يَقُولُ/نَقُولُ`; 58:11 `قِيلَ/قِيلَ`, `تَعْمَلُ`; 58:13 `تَعْمَلُ`; 58:15 `يَعْمَلُ`; 58:16 `ٱتَّخَذُ`; 58:22 `رُوحٍ`; surface anchor unavailable for root `ء م ه`
- Synthesis: Negotiation and reciprocal undertaking turn disputed speech into an obligation-bearing dealing. A claimant can then pursue the resulting right, wrongdoing becomes answerable, and resolution either restores what is due or deliberately remits punishment. This legal micro-scene reframes the opening complaint and its expiation as claim resolution rather than verbal correction alone, while the surah's later accounting supplies the horizon in which no obligation disappears merely because its agent denies or forgets it.

### S9. P1 to P2 - A Work Crew Alternates Shifts and Receives Its Due
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (58:3, 6-7, 11-12) repeatedly attributes known deeds and places value before the hands; P2 (58:13, 15, 22) carries action into standing duties and outcome. The bridge is a repeated work signature: assignment, manual production, alternating turns, attribution to hands, and provision for labor.
- Scene or process: A crew is put to work by hand in digging, clay work, and carpentry; skilled workers alternate turns, their production is assigned to their hands, and wages or provision answer the labor.
- Active motifs: putting another to work `quranic:root_001046:B002/m01`; manual digging and clay crew `quranic:root_001046:B006/m01`; clay workers and carpenter `quranic:root_001167:B003/m01`; skilled craftsperson `quranic:root_001693:B015/m01`; work assigned to the hands `quranic:root_001693:B009/m01`; alternating work shifts `quranic:root_000609:B008/m01`; worker's wage or provision `quranic:root_001046:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:3 `تَعْمَلُ`; 58:6 `عَمِلُ`; 58:7 `عَمِلُ`; 58:11 `تَعْمَلُ`; 58:12 `يَدَىْ`; 58:13 `تَعْمَلُ`, `تَفْعَلُ`, `يَدَىْ`; 58:15 `يَعْمَلُ`; 58:22 `رُوحٍ`
- Synthesis: The repeated vocabulary of deeds acquires the organization of a worksite. Acts are commissioned, materially produced by identifiable hands, distributed across turns, and answered by provision. This materializes divine knowledge and final report as accountability for produced work: neither speech nor allegiance remains disembodied when agency can be assigned to hands and its due can be rendered.

### S10. P1 to P2 - Counted Strands Become a Strengthened Cord
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (58:7, 11) enumerates groups of three, four, and five and then names degrees; P2 (58:15, 21) names severity and strengthening might. The bridge is a craft progression from counted parts through interfolding and knotting to composite strength.
- Scene or process: Three-, four-, and five-strand cords are twisted, folded into one another, secured with a tightened knot, and strengthened part by part.
- Active motifs: three-strand cord `quranic:root_000203:B004/m01`; four-strand cord `quranic:root_000536:B003/m01`; five-strand cord `quranic:root_000439:B005/m01`; interfolded cord strands `quranic:root_000468:B005/m01`; tightened knot and bond `quranic:root_000782:B001/m01`; one part strengthened by another `quranic:root_001008:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:7 `ثَلَٰثَةٍ`, `رَابِعُ`, `خَمْسَةٍ`; 58:11 `دَرَجَٰتٍ`; 58:15 `شَدِيدًا`; 58:21 `عَزِيزٌ`
- Synthesis: The sequence of three, four, and five acquires a precise craft analogue: counted plurality becomes durable only when strands are interfolded, knotted, and mutually reinforcing. The mechanism materializes 58:7's refusal to treat a numbered gathering as a sealed unit and carries that structure toward the surah's final language of strength. It also pressures mere headcount as a basis of solidarity: composite force depends on the relation among parts, not their number alone.

### S11. P1 - Birth Signs Resolve into Purification
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A midwife receives a newborn; membranes, blood, and postpartum discharge mark the birth, after which observed bodily signs distinguish continued bleeding from cessation and washing completes purification.
- Active motifs: midwife receiving the newborn `quranic:root_001198:B007/m01`; recently born child `quranic:root_001683:B004/m01`; membranes and blood emerging at birth `quranic:root_000822:B006/m01`; childbirth and postpartum blood `quranic:root_001533:B005/m01`; uterine discharge following birth `quranic:root_000994:B009/m01`; observed cloth signs distinguishing bleeding from purity `quranic:root_000531:B007/m01`; cessation and washing that complete purification `quranic:root_000953:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:2 `وَلَدْ`; 58:3 `قَبْلِ`; 58:4 `قَبْلِ`, `عَذَابٌ`; 58:5 `قَبْلِ`, `عَذَابٌ`; 58:6 `شَهِيدٌ`; 58:7 `تَرَ`; 58:8 `تَرَ`, `أَنفُسِ`, `يُعَذِّبُ`; 58:12 `أَطْهَرُ`
- Synthesis: The scene gives maternity an evidentiary sequence that speech cannot counterfeit: reception of a newborn is accompanied by bodily signs and a postpartum transition whose completion is observed and washed. It therefore strengthens 58:2's criterion that mothers are those who gave birth and lets the purification of 58:12 resonate as a verified change of state, not a cosmetic relabeling. Zihar fails precisely where embodied evidence succeeds.

### S12. P1 - The Hunter's Blind Cannot Create an Unseen Place
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hunters enter an apparently unobserved wilderness, conceal themselves behind a shooting blind, set snares, deploy an instrument that supplies prey, and take successive quarry.
- Active motifs: empty land where no one hears or sees `quranic:root_000741:B014/m01`; wilderness and desert ground `quranic:root_000104:B005/m01`; hunters setting out for game `quranic:root_000745:B006/m01`; concealing blind for the hunter `quranic:root_000584:B011/m01`; set hunting snares `quranic:root_000880:B005/m01`; hunting instrument that feeds its user `quranic:root_000934:B006/m01`; successive quarry in one release `quranic:root_000993:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:1 `سَمِعَ/يَسْمَعُ/سَمِيعٌۢ`; 58:3 `رَقَبَةٍ`; 58:4 `إِطْعَامُ`; 58:7 `سَّمَٰوَٰتِ`; 58:8 `يَصْلَ`, `عُدْوَٰنِ`; 58:9 `بِرِّ`, `عُدْوَٰنِ`
- Synthesis: The cluster turns harmful secret counsel into an ambush mechanism: remote ground, visual cover, trap, instrument, and repeated capture all depend on the quarry's ignorance and the hunter's imagined invisibility. The opening declaration of hearing and sight, followed by presence within every private grouping, removes the ambusher's decisive advantage. Concealment can organize aggression, but it cannot manufacture a place outside reception and account.

### S13. P1 - Entrusted Care Requires a Sufficient Guardian
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A dependent's affair is entrusted to an agent who accepts responsibility, maintains the ward, watches against harm, administers the work, and stands as guarantor.
- Active motifs: entrusting an affair to another `quranic:root_001681:B001/m01`; sufficient guardian and guarantor `quranic:root_001681:B006/m01`; dependent ward and burden of care `quranic:root_001315:B002/m01`; maintenance and protective governance `quranic:root_001273:B004/m01`; guard and watchman `quranic:root_000584:B002/m01`; appointed administration `quranic:root_001046:B003/m01`; sponsorship and surety `quranic:root_001332:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:3 `رَقَبَةٍ`, `تَعْمَلُ`; 58:6 `عَمِلُ`, `كُلِّ`; 58:7 `عَمِلُ`, `كُلِّ`, `قِيَٰمَةِ`, `يَكُونُ/كَانُ`; 58:10 `يَتَوَكَّلِ`; 58:11 `تَعْمَلُ`
- Synthesis: Reliance in 58:10 is materialized as delegated care rather than inactivity. The entrusted party is sufficient because guardianship combines maintenance, vigilance, administration, and surety around someone unable to carry the burden alone. Within a surah that repeatedly places hidden conduct under knowledge, this social frame makes trust answerable: adequate protection is an office performed, not a title claimed.

### S14. P1 and P2 - Burial Produces the Widow's Ordered Transition
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (58:1-4) presents an attempted marital rupture by utterance and a path back through release and expiation; P2 (58:16-22) activates death and burial senses before the surah's final fixed outcomes. The bridge is a contrast between a failed speech-act of dissolution and the embodied event that actually initiates mourning, waiting, and renewed courtship.
- Scene or process: A husband dies and is covered in burial; his widow relinquishes adornment, waits through the changed status, and only afterward receives approaches from new suitors.
- Active motifs: death expressed as release from life `quranic:root_000609:B016/m01`; covering the dead in grave or shroud `quranic:root_000266:B009/m01`; widow's mourning and abandonment of adornment `quranic:root_000002:B007/m01`; woman waiting after her husband's death `quranic:root_000584:B009/m01`; widow approached by suitors through correspondence `quranic:root_000563:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 58:3 `رَقَبَةٍ`; 58:4 `حُدُودُ`, `رَسُولِ`; 58:5 `يُحَآدُّ`, `رَسُولَ`; 58:16 `جُنَّةً`; 58:20 `يُحَآدُّ`, `رَسُولَ`; 58:22 `جَنَّٰتٍ`, `حَآدَّ`, `رَسُولَ`, `رُوحٍ`
- Synthesis: Death, burial, mourning, waiting, and later courtship form one ordered change of marital status. That real transition usefully pressures the zihar claim: an utterance cannot confer the social condition produced by death and its embodied rites. The surah's opening therefore answers verbal rupture with release, restraint, provision, and restored contact rather than treating the wife as though bereavement had already reorganized her life.
